f. The circumstances under which the country can update the values of the reference indicators- Inc.

The design incorporates an adaptive approach, anticipating that the NDC update in 2030 will allow recalibrate the scheme as the enabling environment and national implementation mature, ensuring that The adjustments to the PAMs should respond to the optimization of the country's aggregate climate contribution and not solely to the monitoring of isolated indicators.

The Dominican Republic may update the values of the reference indicators used in the Present NDC when significant changes occur in information, methodologies, or conditions national factors that affect the consistency or accuracy of these indicators. In particular, the values They may be updated under the following circumstances:

  • Publication of new IPCC methodologies, guidelines or emission factors, or methodological revisions that modify the calculation of the National GHG Inventory or the sectoral baseline;

  • Availability of new official statistical data, improvements in the quality or granularity of the sectoral information, or updating of historical series by governing institutions of national statistical system;

  • Correction of material errors, inconsistencies or gaps identified in the inventories, sectoral projections or models;

  • Significant changes in national circumstances that affect the baseline assumptions —for example, structural variations in the economy, in energy demand, regulatory changes relevant, or extreme events with a significant impact on emitting or sinking sectors—;

  • Adjustments resulting from the integration of new sectors, gases or categories into the scope of the NDC

  • Institutional and regulatory reforms that modify the way in which they are generated, reported or validated climate data within the framework of the National Climate Transparency System (SNTC).

All updates will be carried out in a manner consistent with TACCC principles, with documentation from review process, and will be published within the framework of the ETF reports.

2. Deadlines and/or time periods for implementation

a. Timeframe and/or period for implementation, including start and end dates, of in accordance with any other relevant decision adopted by the Conference of the Parties in its capacity as Meeting of the Parties to the Paris Agreement (CMA).

The medium-term vision for the period January 2026 to December 2030 is defined as part of The implementation phase of the Dominican Republic's NDC 3.0. This period includes the update the NDC Action Plan within a maximum period of twelve months before the end of the NDC. 2026, in order to incorporate enabling frameworks, legal instruments and institutional arrangements that will guide the implementation of climate action goals. This process will be coordinated by a Committee National Board comprised of the Ministry of Finance and Economy, the Ministry of Environment and Natural Resources and the National Council for Climate Change and Carbon Markets, with the participation of institutions from the public sector, private sector, academia, civil society and partners international.

The updated Action Plan will serve as an operational instrument to guide the implementation of measures during the period 2026 to 2030 and will be used as a basis for the preparation of the next Update of the Nationally Determined Contribution. This cycle will allow us to leverage the experience gained in implementation and monitoring. of the progress and evolution of the economic and sectoral context to support the formulation of NDC 4.0 planned for 2030, consistent with national priorities and international commitments.

Likewise, the review process will consider alignment with the Multi-Year Public Investment Plan, the sectoral planning and private sector financing strategies, strengthening the Linking climate planning with development planning. This approach seeks to ensure continuity in climate action and facilitating the transition to the next cycle of ambition climate after 2030.

b. Whether it is a one-year or multi-year objective, as appropriate.

The new commitment of NDC 3.0 RD-2025 presents 26 PAMs (Policies, Actions and Measures) for the mitigation component that will be achieved in a first period (2030) and second period (2035), where They have a GHG indicator that accounts for emissions reductions in the first period and everything related to enabling frameworks, studies and strategies throughout the first and second periods, which cover the mitigation sectors.

The mitigation targets are established in accordance with the NDC Action Plan and there will be an update of the NDC-RD 2025 in 2030 in accordance with the implementation of the system national MRV established and with adjustments to sectors/subsectors for a period of several years.

The mitigation goal corresponds to a two-stage objective (2026-2030) and (2031-2035), covering the Implementation period 2026 to 2035. Progress will be continuously assessed throughout the time horizon, consistent with national monitoring and reporting systems and with the cycles of Reporting under the Enhanced Transparency Framework established in the Paris Agreement. This approach It allows for reflecting the progressive nature of the implementation of policies and measures and the evolution of emissions throughout the commitment period.

3. Scope and coverage

a. General description of the objective.

The Dominican Republic's mitigation objective consists of a reduction in greenhouse gas emissions. greenhouse effect at the economy-wide level in relation to a trend scenario Business As Usual, covering the period 2026 to 2035. The target covers the main emitting gases and sectors according to to the National GHG Inventory and will be implemented through the progressive execution of policies, measures and enabling frameworks aimed at transforming the energy, production and territorial systems of country.

The objective is structured as a multi-year goal with continuous progress assessment, incorporating unconditional and conditional components, subject to the availability of financing, transfer technological and capacity building. Its implementation is based on climate planning. national and in the strategic instruments that guide the transition towards low-risk development emissions and climate resilient.

The NDC 3.0 RD-2025 presents 26 PAMs (Policies, Actions and Measures) for the mitigation component that It will be implemented in the first part of the period (2026-2030) and for the second part of the period (2031-2035). In both, the GHG indicator that accounts for the reduction of emissions in the first period and all the related to enabling frameworks, studies and strategies in the first and second part of the period, which It covers the five mitigation sectors.

The mitigation targets are established in accordance with the NDC Action Plan and there will be an update of the NDC-RD 2025 in 2030 in accordance with the implementation of the system national MRV established and with adjustments to sectors/subsectors for a period of several years.

b. Sectors, gases, categories and groupings covered by the NDC, including, as applicable, from in accordance with the IPCC guidelines.

The Dominican Republic adheres to the classification of emission sectors described by the IPCC: in function of sources and end uses: energy, industrial processes and product uses, Agriculture, land use and forestry, and waste. And their commitment is based on greenhouse gases. estimated and recorded in the country: carbon dioxide [CO2], methane [CH4], nitrous oxide [N2O], hydrofluorocarbons [HFCs].

As a developing country, according to its capabilities, the Dominican Republic is prepared to report four gases (CO2, CH4, N2O and HFCs) in the NDC implementation period 2026-2035, will prepare its capabilities with international support, according to national circumstances, to estimate in a way progressively from the INGEI after 2030 other additional gases (PFCs and SF6s).

The mitigation approach is implemented in the most relevant categories that occur in the country, which They meet both level and trend criteria, such as: Burning activities Fuels - Energy industries, specifically electricity generation and transportation, Minerals industry - Production of cement and lime, Use of substitute products for the substances that They deplete the ozone layer, specifically mentioning Refrigeration and Air Conditioning, Enteric Fermentation, and Manure management, rice cultivation, and solid waste disposal. For emission sources and Sinks within land use and forestry, the main category most relevant by the method turned out to be forest lands that remain as such, as well as those where they provide the most benefits and co-benefits intervention.

c. How the Party has taken into account paragraphs 31(c) and (d) of decision 1/CP.21.

The Dominican Republic has considered paragraphs 31(c) and 31(d) of decision 1/CP.21 by means of structuring your contribution in a way that strengthens clarity, transparency, and understanding of The mitigation objective is achieved through the provision of detailed information in the present ICTU table. The formulation of the objective is based on data from the National GHG Inventory and on methodologies consistent with IPCC guidelines, ensuring coherence with national systems of monitoring, reporting and verification.

The principles of environmental integrity and accuracy were applied in the construction of the target. comparability, consistency, and transparency, in accordance with Article 4.13 of the Paris Agreement. In order to avoid double counting, the shares associated with the previous portfolio were explicitly differentiated. of NDC 2.0 and those corresponding to the new portfolio of NDC 3.0. Likewise, even though the The sectoral classification of policies and measures does not fully coincide with the structure of the inventory, They used methodological standardization criteria that allow for traceability and cross-referencing of impacts in terms of emissions reduction, maintaining consistency with national reports.

Additionally, to strengthen the integrity of the monitoring and prevent risks of double counting or Due to the postponement of implementation, the mitigation impact estimate was based on interventions with identified or programmed funding until 2030. This approach seeks to ensure that the actions prioritized projects will be implemented in the first part of the 2026-2030 implementation period, even if their The results in emissions reductions will be fully realized in the second period, from 2031 to 2035. This criterion contributes to the temporal robustness of the objective and reinforces the credibility of the trajectory. mitigation and environmental integrity of the approach adopted.

This approach ensures the comparability of information over time and its alignment with the reporting processes under the Enhanced Transparency Framework, facilitating future updates to the Contribution Nationally Determined.

d. Mitigation co-benefits resulting from the Parties' adaptation actions and/or plans economic diversification, including a description of specific projects, measures, and initiatives the adaptation actions and/or economic diversification plans of the Parties.

The Dominican Republic has considered paragraphs 31(c) and 31(d) of decision 1/CP.21 in structuring its contribution in a way that strengthens the clarity, transparency and understanding of the mitigation objective through the provision of detailed information in the ICTU table. The formulation of the objective is based on data from the National GHG Inventory and methodologies consistent with IPCC guidelines, ensuring consistency with national monitoring, reporting, and verification systems. In this process Principles of environmental integrity, accuracy, comparability, and consistency were applied in line with the Article 4.13 of the Paris Agreement, explicitly differentiating the portfolio actions of NDC 2.0 and NDC 3.0 to avoid double counting. When the sectoral classification of policies and measures does not It coincides with the inventory structure; methodological homologation criteria were applied to to ensure the traceability and comparability of the impacts on emissions reduction.

4. Planning Process

a. Information on the planning processes undertaken by the Party to prepare its NDC and, if it is available, regarding the Party's implementation plans.

  1. Domestic institutional arrangements, participation and public engagement with local communities and the indigenous peoples, in a gender-sensitive manner;

  2. Contextual issues, including, but not limited to, as appropriate:

    1. National circumstances, such as geography, climate, economy, sustainable development and eradication of poverty;

    2. Good practices and experience related to the preparation of the determined contribution at the level national;

    3. Other contextual aspirations and priorities recognized upon joining the Paris Agreement;

      Additionally, to strengthen the integrity of the monitoring and reduce the risk of delays in the Implementation, the mitigation impact estimate was based on interventions with financing identified or programmed until 2030. This approach seeks to ensure that the actions prioritized projects will be implemented in the first part of the 2026-2030 period, even though their reduction benefits emissions will fully materialize between 2031 and 2035. Overall, this approach strengthens the credibility of the mitigation trajectory ensures the temporal comparability of the information and It maintains alignment with reporting processes under the Enhanced Transparency Framework and with future NDC updates.

The preparation of the Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC 3.0 RD-2025) is based on the commitments made in the DR 2020 NDC and the progress achieved during its implementation. The process of The update was led by the NDC 3.0 Steering Committee, comprised of the National Development Council Climate Change and Carbon Markets, the Ministry of Finance and Economy and the Ministry of the Environment Environment and Natural Resources, with the technical and financial support of the Inter-American Development Bank, the United Nations Development Programme and other strategic partners.

From February to November 2025, a participatory, inclusive, and technically sound process was developed. robust that included:

  • Two NDC Dialogues: focused on the review of mitigation and adaptation measures, and on validation of the climate transparency component (Strengthened Transparency Framework MRT).

  • Six cross-cutting workshops: addressing issues of childhood, youth, gender, climate empowerment (ACE), private sector and local communities.

  • Sectoral working groups: with the participation of more than 20 public, private and other institutions international cooperation to validate sectoral mitigation and adaptation goals.

  • Ad-hoc bilateral consultations: with key institutions to strengthen the technical contextualization of the measures.

  • Public consultation form: which allowed for the collection of input from technical, academic and other stakeholders citizens.

    This approach made it possible to integrate sectoral mitigation PAMs (Policies, Actions and Measures), with a estimated reduction of approximately 9,997.37 ktCO₂eq annually by 2035, and strengthen the component adaptation through measures with broad potential for co-benefits.

    Elements for the implementation of NDC 3.0-RD 2025

    • Implementation will be coordinated with the National Development Strategy 2030, the META strategy 2036, and the NDC Action Plan, which will be updated periodically.

    • Enabling frameworks will be strengthened, including technical standards (NORDOM), schemes of conformity assessment and climate transparency mechanisms (SNTC).

    • Private sector participation will be promoted through the analysis of incentives and mechanisms collaborative, capacity building and monitoring schemes with specific indicators.

    • Cross-cutting approaches such as gender, childhood, youth and ACE will be integrated into all measures.

      • Unconditional and conditional commitments

      • Unconditional: These will be achieved through the reorientation of public investments and strengthening institutional, and promotion of low-carbon technologies.

      • Conditional: They will be implemented with international financial support, including the Green Fund for Climate change (GCF), multilateral banking, foreign direct investment, and technical cooperation specialized. A comprehensive assessment of technological capabilities and needs will be carried out. country.

Specific information applicable to the Parties, including regional organizations of economic integration and its member states, which have reached an agreement to act jointly in accordance with Article 4, paragraph 2, of the Paris Agreement, including the Parties that agreed to act jointly and the terms of the agreement, in accordance with the Article 4, paragraphs 16-18, of the Paris Agreement.

Not applicable.

c. How the preparation of your NDC Part has been informed by the balance sheet results global, in accordance with Article 4, paragraph 9, of the Paris Agreement.

The preparation of the Nationally Determined Contribution was informed by the results from the first global assessment under the Paris Agreement, which highlight the need to strengthen climate ambition, accelerate the energy transition and expand the implementation of mitigation and adaptation measures. These results were reflected in the prioritization of actions aimed at transforming energy systems, productive and territorial aspects, as well as in the strengthening of enabling frameworks and institutional frameworks for its implementation.

Furthermore, the update process took into account the importance noted in the overall balance sheet to mobilize climate finance, promote the inclusion of non-state actors and improve The coherence between climate planning and national development. In this context, the NDC incorporates multi-sectoral participation mechanisms, integration with economic planning and strengthening of climate transparency, aligning with the global balance sheet's call to improve implementation and monitoring of collective progress.

d. Each Party with an NDC that includes adaptation actions and/or diversification plans economic benefits that result in secondary mitigation benefits consistent with Article 4, paragraph 7.

  1. What have been the economic and social consequences of the response measures considered in the development of the nationally determined contribution;

  2. Specific projects, measures and activities to be implemented to contribute benefits secondary mitigation measures, including information on adaptation plans that also contribute mitigation co-benefits, which may cover, but are not limited to, key sectors such as energy, resources, water resources, coastal resources, human and urban settlements planning, agriculture and forestry; and economic diversification actions, which may cover, but are not They are limited to sectors such as manufacturing and industry, energy and mining, transportation and Communication, Construction, Tourism, Real Estate, Agriculture and Fishing.

The Dominican Republic's Nationally Determined Contribution incorporates actions of adaptation that generates mitigation co-benefits consistent with Article 4, paragraph 7, of the Paris Agreement. While the Dominican Republic does not have specific plans to economic diversification, integrates measures aimed at strengthening resilience in key sectors that would support economic diversification and sustainable management of natural resources, land-use planning and resilient infrastructure, which They indirectly contribute to improving the efficiency of energy and resource use and reducing pressures on emissions and technological penetration.

These actions include initiatives related to the sustainable management of ecosystems, resilient production practices and sectoral planning approaches that, even when They are primarily aimed at improving sector indicators, and also support the reduction of climate vulnerabilities, generating additional benefits in terms of mitigation through optimization of input use, reduction of losses and Strengthening natural sinks. In this way, the NDC promotes integration consistent between adaptation and mitigation without establishing specific instruments of economic diversification.

5. Assumptions and methodological approaches, including those for estimating and accounting for the anthropogenic GHG emissions and removals, as applicable.

a. Assumptions and methodological approaches used to account for emissions and absorptions anthropogenic GHG emissions corresponding to the country's NDC, in accordance with the decision 1/CP.21, paragraph 31, and the accounting guidance adopted by the CMA.

Based on the reviewed information and the support received through technical assistance Sectorally, the process was structured with a national timeline that began with a workshop of launch and continued with a series of sectoral technical dialogues focused on Identify and propose mitigation actions in the various sectors. identified with the technical support of the Inter-American Development Bank and other partners of Cooperation was evaluated using a narrative methodology structured by the team coordinator, through bilateral technical sessions with national actors, culminating in the discussion of results within the process of updating NDC 3.0 RD 2025 with the members of the Steering Committee.

The draft of the mitigation component of NDC 3.0 RD 2025 was submitted for public feedback to the chain of sector actors, granting a deadline for their validation by national authorities and its presentation at a final consultation workshop before October 2025. This process involved the participation of public authorities, academia, civil society organizations, the private sector, and business associations, addressing means of implementation, cross-cutting elements and alignment with the Objectives of Sustainable Development.

Elements considered for the first implementation period:

The objectives are expected to be achieved through coordination with the National Development Strategy, the DECCC Plan and the NDC Action Plan 2026 to 2030, which will be developed in the first quarter of 2026 and updated periodically to incorporate PAMs (Policies, Actions and Measures) of more precise mitigation in the period 2026 to 2030.

  • The unconditional objective will be achieved through incentives and the reorientation of public investments and private entities towards low-emission activities, supported by capacity assessments national and financial and technological needs.

  • The conditional objective includes the strengthening of enabling frameworks, development of long-term studies and mobilization of financial mechanisms through partnerships public-private partnerships to drive climate action.

  • Capacity building measures and technical assistance will be implemented for the sector private sector initiatives in climate finance, aimed at facilitating investments in projects low emissions and resilient to climate change.

    • b. Assumptions and methodological approaches used to account for the implementation of policies and measures or strategies in the NDC.

    The accounting for the implementation of PAMs (Policies, Actions and Measures) included in the The Nationally Determined Contribution is realized through the systematic monitoring of its progress through institutional arrangements and reporting mechanisms established at the level national. A measure is considered to be under implementation when there is evidence verifiable implementation, including regulatory approval, budget allocation, and deployment This monitoring is based on the implementation of programs or institutional progress reports. official information generated by the responsible institutions and in the documentation available in the sectoral planning processes and climate.

    The methodological approach was based on the 2022-2025 Action Plan (PDA NDC 2022-2023) as The evaluation at the end of 2023 was taken as a reference instrument for the monitoring progress, as well as in the development of the planned NDC Implementation Plan for the current year, which will allow the goals to be operationalized through the definition of institutional responsibilities, timelines, and monitoring mechanisms. These instruments These are complemented by progress indicators, inter-institutional technical reviews, and national climate transparency mechanisms, integrated into the reporting processes of Biennial Transparency Report in accordance with the Enhanced Transparency Framework. This This approach ensures the traceability of the measures and maintains consistency between implementation cycles. This avoids double counting between NDC portfolios, facilitating continuous assessment. of progress in national climate action.

    c. If applicable, information on how the country will take into account the methods and the existing guidelines under the Convention for accounting for emissions and removals anthropogenic, in accordance with Article 4, paragraph 14 of the Paris Agreement, according to as appropriate.

    The Dominican Republic will take into account the existing methods and guidelines under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change for the accounting of Anthropogenic emissions and removals of greenhouse gases. The estimates are They are based on the applicable IPCC Guidelines and the information generated through the National GHG Inventory, ensuring consistency with the reporting processes developed in National communications, biennial reports, and the Biennial Transparency Report. Information management is carried out through national institutional arrangements of existing inter-institutional reporting and coordination, while efforts continue to Strengthening and formalizing national climate transparency systems.

    The accounting associated with the Nationally Determined Contribution maintains consistency with the time series of the national inventory, incorporating recalculations where appropriate to reflect methodological or informational improvements, and using internationally recognized metrics recognized standards that allow for intertemporal comparability. The treatment of emissions and Land use sector absorptions are carried out in accordance with the approaches established under the Convention, ensuring consistency between inventories and accounting processes of the NDC. This approach facilitates alignment with the Paris Agreement and the Enhanced Framework of Transparency, strengthening the robustness and continuity of national emissions monitoring and absorptions.

    d. IPCC methodologies and metrics used to estimate anthropogenic emissions and removals GHG emissions.

    Estimates of anthropogenic greenhouse gas emissions and removals are They carried out using the IPCC 2006 Guidelines for National GHG Inventories and their 2019 Refinement, consistent with the information reported in the National Inventory. The methods applied are based on the approaches methodological factors corresponding to the availability of national data, using IPCC default emission factors when No country-specific information is available. The treatment of absorptions is It is carried out in accordance with the sectoral approaches established in said guidelines.

    Emissions are expressed in carbon dioxide equivalent using potential metrics of Atmospheric Warming based on the IPCC Sixth Assessment Report. This This approach ensures consistency with national reporting processes and allows for international comparability of the estimates used for monitoring the Nationally Determined Contribution.

    e. Assumptions, methodologies and approaches specific to the sector, category or activity, consistent with the IPCC's guidance, and integration of national agencies with a country database.

    1. Approach to addressing emissions and subsequent removals from nature disturbances on managed lands;

    2. Approach used to account for emissions and removals of felled timber and their products;

    3. Approach used to address the effects of age-class structure in forests;

    The estimation of emissions and absorptions incorporates assumptions, methodologies, and approaches specific to sector and activity category in accordance with IPCC guidelines, adapted to national circumstances and following the analytical scheme of the Inventory Most recent national GHG data. Sectoral data from statistics are used. national, institutional platforms, national and international repositories, as well as information contained in sector plans, diagnoses and relevant technical documentation. In most cases, IPCC default emission factors are applied, supplemented with national factors when information is available. The methodologies applied reflect sectoral differences in terms of data sources, assumptions, and techniques estimate, based on the nature of the activities in energy, industrial processes, agriculture, waste and land use.

    Institutional data integration is achieved through inter-institutional agreements and Technical consultations with experts for mapping and validating information and documentation. Sectoral institutions participate within a solid organizational structure that operates at the national, sectoral and territorial levels, generating inputs that are subsequently integrated Two in national estimates through technical coordination processes. The information is It draws on national databases, including official statistics and repositories. institutional databases and databases of programs and projects supported by organizations multilateral. This approach allows for the consolidation and consistent use of information to at the national level, maintaining methodological consistency with inventory and reporting processes international.

    f. Other assumptions and methodological approaches used to understand the NDC and, where appropriate, estimate the corresponding emissions and removals, including:

    (i) How benchmark indicators, baselines and/or levels are constructed reference, including, where applicable, industry, category or activity-specific reference levels, including, for example: key parameters, assumptions, definitions, methodologies, data sources, and models used.

    (ii) For Parties with NDCs that contain non-GHG components, information on assumptions and methodological approaches used in relation to those components, as appropriate.

    (iii) For climate drivers included in the NDCs not covered by the IPCC guidelines, information on how climate forcing factors are estimated.

    (iv) Additional technical information, as needed.

    The construction of the reference indicators and the baseline is based on the historical series of the National GHG Inventory and in the development of projections of emissions were analyzed using statistical methods applied to observed trends. Key parameters derived from national sectoral data, assumptions consistent with the evolution expected evolution of economic and technological variables, and sources of information from official statistics, sectoral technical documentation and national repositories and international. The methodologies used include trend analysis and regressions. statistics, benchmarking of scenarios, and prioritization tools such as marginal abatement cost curves, ensuring consistency with inventory approaches national.

    The NDC incorporates components not directly associated with the emissions estimate, including non-GHG targets, adaptation actions and environmental strengthening measures enabling institutional, regulatory, and financial environment. These elements contribute to the comprehensive implementation of the contribution and the achievement of structural transformations, but They are not included in the quantitative estimate of emissions and removals. Their monitoring is It is carried out through implementation indicators, institutional milestones, and mechanisms of monitoring established in the NDC Action Plan and in national reporting processes climatic.

    The NDC does not include additional climate forcing factors not covered by the guidelines of the IPCC, therefore specific methodologies do not apply in this area. For your information Additionally, the consistency between the baseline used and the series is highlighted. national inventory, as well as the differentiation between intervention portfolios of different NDC cycles to avoid double counting and ensure temporal comparability of results.

    g. The intention to utilize voluntary cooperation under Article 6 of the Paris Agreement, if applicable.

    The Dominican Republic does not contemplate participation in international carbon markets Under Article 6 of the Paris Agreement for the fulfillment of its NDC 3.0, the country declares explicitly stating that it does not contemplate the use of carbon markets to achieve its goals nationals. Notwithstanding the foregoing, the Dominican Republic participates in a voluntary in the mechanisms of Article 6 of the Paris Agreement, which include the approaches cooperatives of Article 6.2 and the Paris Agreement Crediting Mechanism (PACM) of Article 6.4 for purposes other than this NDC. In this context, the country prioritizes the application of the criterion of additionality, high standards of environmental integrity, transparency in transactions tions and prevent double accounting.

    The mitigation outcomes intended for international transfers, both under the Article 6 mechanisms (ITMOs) as under the CDM, will come from activities and GHGs that do not are included in the NDC and will use a national registry (currently in preparation under the coordination of the CNCCMC) and a unified monitoring mechanism, in order to ensure the coherence between instruments.

    6. As the Party considers its NDC to be fair and ambitious under the circumstances nationals.

    a. How the Party considers its NDC to be fair and ambitious under the circumstances nationals.

    The Dominican Republic considers its Nationally Determined Contribution to be fair and ambitious in light of its national circumstances, taking into account its limited historical contribution to global emissions, its status as a highly developing country vulnerable to the impacts of climate change and its economic and social development priorities. The formulation of the NDC balances the need to advance climate action with the reality of financial, technological, and institutional constraints, reflecting an effort proportional to its national capacities and aligned with the principles of the Paris Agreement.

    The ambition of the contribution is reflected in the strengthening of the sectoral scope of the measures, the integration of enabling frameworks and the incorporation of instruments of planning that allows for progressive implementation towards a development trajectory Low emissions and climate resilient. Furthermore, the differentiation between components unconditional and conditional demonstrate the country's commitment to making progress in mitigation within its capabilities, while seeking to expand its level of ambition through access to international support, consistent with the principle of common but differentiated responsibilities and respective capabilities.

    b. Equity considerations, including a reflection on fairness.

    The Dominican Republic addresses equity in the context of its Nationally Determined Contribution Determined by considering its limited historical contribution to global emissions and its high vulnerability to the impacts of climate change. This reflection It is based on the recognition of differences in national capabilities and needs. development and exposure to climate risks, guiding the definition of compatible actions with sustainable economic growth, the reduction of inequalities and the strengthening of social and environmental resilience.

    The equity perspective is integrated across the NDC by promoting inclusive approaches that consider gender equality, youth participation and childhood, strengthening education and climate empowerment, and inclusion territorial rights of local communities. Likewise, a just transition is promoted that recognizes the social and economic implications of climate change, along with the participation of multiple actors in the planning and implementation processes. The distinction between unconditional and conditional components also reflects the importance of equitable access to financing, technology and international cooperation as an element key to sustaining and expanding national climate action.

    c. How the Party has addressed Article 4, paragraph 3, of the Paris Agreement.

    The Dominican Republic has addressed Article 4, paragraph 3, of the Paris Agreement by ensuring that its Nationally Determined Contribution represents a progression with respect to the previous contribution and reflect the highest possible level of ambition based on your national circumstances. The update incorporates improvements in sector coverage, the strengthening enabling frameworks and broader integration of instruments planning that allows climate action to be directed towards a structural transformation long term.

    Furthermore, the contribution broadens the methodological and technical approach used in cycles previous ones, incorporating more robust mitigation potential analyses, prioritization of monitoring measures and mechanisms, along with a clear distinction between unconditional and conditional components. This approach reflects the intention of country to progressively advance in climate action, maintaining consistency with its institutional capacities, development needs and access to international support.

    d. How the Party has addressed Article 4, paragraph 4, of the Paris Agreement.

    The Dominican Republic has addressed Article 4, paragraph 4, of the Paris Agreement by formulating a Nationally Determined Contribution that progressively strengthens and expands its mitigation efforts based on its circumstances as a developing country. This contribution encompasses multiple sectors of the economy and promotes the implementation of policies and measures aimed at reducing emissions and strengthening frameworks enabling factors that facilitate the transformation towards low-carbon development.

    Furthermore, the NDC reflects progress compared to previous cycles by expanding the scope of actions, strengthening of climate planning and integration of mechanisms that allow for improved progress monitoring. This approach highlights the country's commitment to continue increasing its contribution to global climate action gradually and consistently with its institutional capacities, development needs and access to international support.

    e. How the Party has addressed Article 4, paragraph 6 of the Paris Agreement.

    The Dominican Republic has addressed Article 4, paragraph 6, of the Paris Agreement recognizing the importance of considering national circumstances and capabilities in the formulation of its Nationally Determined Contribution. In this context, the NDC integrates not only mitigation objectives, but also strategies, plans, and actions that guide their implementation, reflecting a flexible approach adapted to institutional realities, financial and technological sectors of the country.

    The contribution also incorporates climate planning instruments and frameworks. enabling factors and inter-institutional coordination mechanisms that allow progress in action climate change in a progressive manner. This approach recognizes the structural limitations and the capacity-building needs, while maintaining the country's commitment to continually improve their climate ambition and their contribution to the global effort.

    7. How does the NDC contribute to achieving the objective of the Convention as set out in its Article 2.

    a. How the NDC contributes to achieving the objective of the Convention established in its Article 2.

    The Dominican Republic's Nationally Determined Contribution contributes to the achievement of objective of the Convention through the implementation of actions aimed at reduction progressive reduction of greenhouse gas emissions and strengthening of resilience in response to the impacts of climate change, the NDC promotes the transformation of sectors key to the economy through policies, measures and enabling frameworks that favor a low-emission and climate-resilient development trajectory, consistent with the principles of sustainable development.

    Likewise, although the emissions from the Dominican Republic are not representative in the In a global context, our contribution supports the international collective effort to stabilizing greenhouse gas concentrations by integrating mitigation, adaptation and institutional strengthening in its national climate planning. This This approach recognizes shared responsibility for global climate action and positions the country as an active part of the effort to prevent dangerous interference in the system climate, while simultaneously maintaining its economic and social development priorities.

    b. How the NDC contributes to Article 2, paragraph 1(a), and Article 4, paragraph 1, of the Paris Agreement.

    The Dominican Republic's Nationally Determined Contribution contributes to compliance with Article 2, paragraph 1(a), of the Paris Agreement through the implementation of actions aimed at reducing greenhouse gas emissions and strengthening the climate resilience, contributing to the global effort to limit the temperature increase global media. Through the progressive transformation of strategic sectors, the Strengthening enabling frameworks and promoting cleaner technologies, the NDC supports a development trajectory consistent with international climate goals.

    Furthermore, the contribution responds to Article 4, paragraph 1, by promoting the reduction progressive reduction of emissions and strengthening of natural sinks, consistent with The evolution of national capabilities. The PAMs (Policies, Actions and Measures) The measures contemplated allow for a gradual shift towards a sustained reduction in emissions and towards a balance between emissions and absorption, integrating mitigation, adaptation and Institutional strengthening as part of long-term climate planning.

    Annex 1. Narrative/tabular formats for the new action goals of the mitigation component in NDC 3.0 RD-2025

    ENERGY SECTOR

    Current situation and GHG emissions for the Sector

    The Energy sector includes categories with the highest number of emissions, such as Power Generation Electricity and Transportation in the Dominican Republic, it can be seen that the use of fuels in Stationary and mobile applications are the sources with the greatest potential for GHG emissions. Exploration and exploitation of primary resources does not occur in a very significant way, since the Most of the fuel used in the country is imported, and much of this fuel is in primary source form, therefore it is necessary to transform it.

    The trend of GHG emissions at the end of 2022 from the Energy sector were totaled at 27,622.8 ktCO2, 32.9 ktCH4 and 0.6 ktN2O using the AR5-IPCC. Regarding the Emissions of precursor gases were 47.7 ktNOx, 160.3 ktCO, 24.1 ktCOVDM and 88.6 ktSO2.

Data from the National Long-Term Program The deadline for the period January 2025-December 2029 was published by the OC in December 2024. In addition to a Review of the provisional and definitive concessions published by the CNE-2024. The factor of emission for the National Interconnected Electric System (SENI).

    Activities required for the adaptation of the Goal

    Description of the main activities during the execution period, to complete the goal:

    ü Construction of 30 solar parks projecting a capacity of 1,267.15 MWn and of these 170 MW in storage systems (BESS).

    • Construction of 5 (five) 250 MWn wind farms. • Construction of a power plant 12 MWn hydroelectric plant.

    ü Streamline the signing of long-term power purchase agreements (PPAs) and increase the applications and concessions for renewable energy projects.

    • Maintain appropriate incentives for investments, ensuring compensation for expected ecological and economic benefits of renewable energies in the country.

    Observations

    Energy, category: (1.A.1.a.) Energy industries. (1.A.1.a.) Main activity Electricity and heat production

    CO2, CH4 and N2O

    380,000,000.00 USD

    Brief description of the option

    Reduction of Losses in the Distribution System of the National Electric System The National Interconnected System (SENI) has the capacity to partially increase the availability of energy in a BAU scenario that would be produced by the set of plants that are located feeding the SENI.

    Medium and Low Voltage Network Improvement Program Customer Normalization National Electricity Distribution Companies) IBRD LOAN NUMBER 9624-DO ID of project P180512.

    ü Network Expansion and Electrical Loss Reduction Program in Distribution (DR-L1128).

    Proposal of methodologies and/or methods for monitoring

    The project applies a methodological approach consistent with the 2006 IPCC Guidelines, given that emission reductions are estimated based on the decrease in losses of energy in the distribution networks, multiplied by network emission factors electrical. This logic is fully consistent with the CDM (CDM/UNFCCC) methodologies. applicable to energy efficiency in electrical systems, in particular the methodologies AMS-II.D and AMS-II.E , used for loss reduction projects in distribution networks and replacement of inefficient teams. These methodologies establish the determination of a scenario of reference (technical losses without project), the scenario with project (reduced losses), and emission reductions as the difference in energy avoided multiplied by the network emission factor.

    Activities required for the adaptation of the Goal

    Description of the main activities during the execution period to complete the goal: ü It will support the improvement of governance in distribution companies;

    ü Improvement of the measurement infrastructure;

    ü Improvement of the network rehabilitation works;

    ü Application of software and technology solutions.

    Observations

    INDUSTRIAL PROCESSES AND PRODUCT USE SECTOR (IPPU)

    Current situation and emissions.

    The industrial sector of the Dominican Republic has shown significant growth in the recent years. According to the 2022 Yearbook of Economic Statistics (ONE), the Gross Domestic Product (GDP) The industrial sector experienced a notable increase in 2022. In terms of participation in the In 2022, the industrial sector accounted for approximately 33.8% of the national GDP.

    In the IPPU sector, 5,049.1 kt CO2 eq were generated in 2022. Of this total, the two largest categories Significant GHG emissions represent 98.2%, which are associated with industry of the minerals which generated 2,328.5 kt CO2 eq (46.1%), and on the other hand the related category with the use of substitute products for ozone-depleting substances 2,360.1 kt CO2 eq (46.7%).

    The goals/actions for the initiatives are shown below in narrative and tabular formats. Sectoral mitigation measures obtained from various studies (Technical Assistance) and bilateral meetings with the actors in the sector INDUSTRIAL PROCESSES AND PRODUCT USE (IPPU) in the categories of cement production and the use of substitute products for the substances that deplete the ozone layer respectively, for the process of improving and updating the NDCs 3.0-2025 for specified categories.

    In this context, the Agriculture sector addresses GHG emissions from agricultural activities that They follow a management process, food production, and management practices such as Fertilization, soil management, animal management, among others. In particular, in this This sector can generate emissions of CO2, CH4 and N2O, in addition to precursor gases (NOx, CO, COVDM and SO2).

    UTCUTS SECTOR: FOREST COVER LANDS

    Goal Title: ACHIEVE EFFECTIVE CONSERVATION OF 30% OF THE NATIONAL TERRITORY, Reforesting 4,500km2 of restored degraded forest within APS, and ensuring the detention HABITAT LOSS. (DOMINICAN REPUBLIC 30 X 30)

    Objective/Goal Indicator

    Increase GHG absorption through the reforestation of 4,500 km2 by 2030.

    Responsible Entity (Institution that monitors, reports and verifies the option)

    Type of Instrument (GHG Target, Non-GHG Target and Enabling Framework)

    Status (In planning, approved, in execution)

    Sector and Categories according to IPCC2006

    (Identify specific sector and categories)

    Gases (GHG) (Direct Gases

    and Indirect reported)

    Estimated Financing (Expressed in USD)

    Ministry of Environment and Natural Resources National Council for Climate Change and Carbon Market (CNCCMC).

    Goal: Increase absorptions from 1.35 MtCO₂/year at a rate of 3 tCO₂/ha/year

    GHG Goal: Reduction

    1,350,000 t CO2/year

    Planning: (2024-2030)

    Execution: (2026-2030)

    Land Use and Land Use Change Sector, Category: Forest Land Subcategory 1: Forest Land Remaining Forest Land

    CO2

    471,000,000 USD by 2030

    Brief description of the option

    The measure consists of the effective conservation of 30% of the national territory through the Strengthening the National System of Protected Areas (SINAP), with emphasis on the restoration of approximately 4,500 km² of degraded forests within Protected Areas and halting the net loss of habitat, including critical ecosystems such as mangroves, reefs and rainforests. This action seeks to increase carbon absorption and to guarantee the provision of key ecosystem services, such as water regulation, coastal protection and biodiversity, contributing to the global goal 30x30 and compliance with the NDC.

    Proposal of methodologies and/or methods for monitoring

    The measure of reforestation in Protected Areas under the framework of the initiative Dominican Republic 30x30 It consists of the restoration of approximately 4,500 km² of degraded forests within the National System of Protected Areas (SINAP), with the objective of increasing the capture and carbon storage, strengthening the provision of essential ecosystem services (water regulation, biodiversity, soils) and contribute to climate resilience. This The action is reported in the Land Use and Land Use Change and Forestry sector (UTCUTS), Forest Land category, mainly in the subcategory Forest Land Remaining Forest Land by improving carbon stocks in existing forests and, in some cases, in Land Converted to Forest Land when restoration is applied to previously degraded or deforested lands. A conservative approach, the restoration of these surfaces could generate net absorptions from 0.9 to 2.25 MtCO₂eq annually (approximately 5.4 to 13.5 MtCO₂eq accumulated by 2030), depending on the effective catch rate, according to IPCC 2006/2013 methodologies Wetlands Supplement and reporting frameworks such as CDM AR-ACM0003/AMS0007 and ART-TREES for monitoring and verification.

    Activities required for the adaptation of the Goal

    Planned activities:

    • Planning and zoning: identification of priority restoration areas within Protected Areas and definition of specific management plans;

    ü Ecological restoration: implementation of reforestation and natural regeneration programs assisted, with native species adapted to each ecosystem;

    Management and surveillance: strengthening the management of SINAP, patrolling and control for prevent deforestation, fires and illegal activities within protected areas;

    Monitoring and MRV: establishment of permanent plots, use of remote sensing and protocols biomass and carbon measurement following IPCC/UNFCCC guidelines;

    Community participation: incorporation of local communities into activities of restoration, maintenance and sustainable use of resources;

    Sustainable financing: mobilization of public funds, international cooperation and results-based financing mechanisms (e.g., blue carbon and forestry);

    • Institutional and legal strengthening: updating regulations and strengthening of technical capabilities of protected area managers.

    Observations

    Any information that helps clarify the commitments of each Central Agency Responsible: Ministry of Environment and Natural Resources.

    UTCUTS SECTOR: FOREST COVER LANDS

    Goal Title: ACHIEVE EFFECTIVE CONSERVATION OF 30% OF THE NATIONAL TERRITORY, HALTING THE NET LOSS OF MANGROVES WITHIN APS, AND ENSURING THE HALTING OF LOSS OF HABITAT. (DOMINICAN REPUBLIC 30 X 30)

    Objective/Goal Indicator

    Increase GHG absorptions from the protection of mangroves located in Protected Areas with a 0% Loss of Mangrove swamp by 2030. (Baseline: Net loss of ~10km2 of mangroves between 2015 and 2022)

    Responsible Entity (Institution that monitors, reports and verifies the option)

    Type of Instrument (GHG Target, Non-GHG Target and Enabling Framework)

    Status (In planning, approved, in execution)

    Sector and Categories according to IPCC-2006 (Identify specific sector and categories)

    Gases (GHG) (Reported Direct and Indirect Gases)

    Estimated Financing (Expressed in USD)

    Ministry of Environment and Natural Resources National Council for

    Climate Change and Carbon Market (CNCCMC).

    GHG Target: Absorption/Reduction of 660,000 t CO2 eq

    Planning (2025-2030)

    Execution: (2026-2030)

    Land Use, Land Use Change and Forestry Sector (LULUCF) Forest Land, subcategory Wetlands.

    CO2

    2,000,000,000.00 USD

    Brief description of the option

    The measure seeks ensure the effective conservation of 30% of the national territory , with emphasis on the protection of mangroves located within Protected Areas guaranteeing the zero percent net loss of this ecosystem by 2030 and contributing to halting habitat loss in critical ecosystems. The action will allow maintain and increase blue carbon stocks to strengthen the coastal resilience , preserve the associated biodiversity and contribute to the international goals of biodiversity and climate change (30x30, NDC and SDGs).

    Proposal of methodologies and/or methods for monitoring

    The measure will be quantified and monitored through the application of methodologies Recognized by the CDM/UNFCCC and the IPCC guidelines. For mangrove restoration and management The AR-AMS0003 methodology will be used, while for forest restoration Degraded areas within Protected Areas will be subject to AR-ACM0003 and AR-AMS0007. In terms of International guidelines will be used, specifically the 2006 IPCC Guidelines for the AFOLU sector. and the Wetlands Supplement 2013 to account for carbon stocks in mangroves, both in biomass as in soils. Monitoring will be based on the establishment of permanent plots for measuring aboveground and belowground biomass, soil sampling at depths of 30–100 cm, and the use of satellite imagery and drones to verify coverage and detecting habitat loss. Community participation will be incorporated into surveillance and information gathering activities will be carried out, and QA/QC and external verification protocols aligned with jurisdictional frameworks will be applied, such as ART-TREES. The proposed frequency includes annual coverage assessments and measurements. in plots every 3–5 years, soil sampling every 5–10 years, and consolidated reports in the BTR/NDC cycles.

    Activities required for the adaptation of the Goal

    Planned activities:

    ü Identify and map priority areas of mangroves and critical habitats within of SINAP;

    ü Implement hydrological restoration and reforestation programs with native species in Degraded mangroves; ü Strengthen surveillance and control in Protected Areas for prevent illegal landfilling, logging, fires and uses;

    • Establish permanent plots and biomass and carbon monitoring systems in mangroves and associated forests; ü Develop a satellite monitoring and GIS system for tracking coverage and early detection of habitat loss;

    ü Involve local communities and grassroots organizations in conservation activities, restoration and participatory reporting;

    • Mobilize sustainable financing (public funds, international cooperation, mechanisms) of blue carbon and forestry); ü Train and equip technical staff and park rangers of SINAP for the implementation and monitoring of the goal;

    • Establish a periodic reporting scheme linked to the BTR/NDC cycles for document progress and results.

    Observations

    Any information that helps clarify the commitments of each Central Agency Responsible: Ministry of Environment and Natural Resources.

    WASTE SECTOR

    Current situation and emissions.

    According to the 2006 IPCC Guidelines, GHG emissions are considered in this sector. generated by the handling and final disposal of waste, whether solid or liquid. Wastewater originates from a variety of domestic, commercial, and industrial sources and They can be treated on-site (not collected), or transferred via sewerage to a central facility (collected), or disposed of without treatment nearby or through drains. The main The precursor for CH4 generation in the sector is the carbon fraction in organic matter susceptible to decomposition by the action of microorganisms; these decompose the carbon fraction of organic matter by converting it into CH4.

    For the year 2022, the reported emissions in this sector were 5,149.0 ktCO2 eq. Where the two The heavier categories represent 99.3%, of which 4,463.1 ktCO2 eq (86.7%) were generated by the solid waste disposal category, and 646.5 ktCO2 eq (12.6%) for treatment and discharge of wastewater.

    The goals/actions for the initiatives are shown below in narrative and tabular formats. Sectoral mitigation measures obtained from various studies (Technical Assistance) and bilateral meetings with the actors in the sector WASTE in the categories of Solid Waste Disposal and Wastewater Treatment, for the process of improvement and update of the NDC 3.0-2025 for specified categories.

    Status (In planning, approved, in execution)

    Sector and Categories according to IPCC-2006 (Identify specific sector and categories)

    Gases (GHG) (Reported Direct and Indirect Gases)

    Estimated Financing (Expressed in USD)

    MMARN

    Sustainable DO

    Dominican Municipal League

    FEDOMU INDOCAL

    GHG Goal: Reduction of 138,546.23 t CO2eq/year

    Planning (2020-2024)

    Planned Implementation (2026-2030)

    Waste Sector, Category: 4A. Solid waste disposal.

    CH4

    Total, for a period of 5 years: 110,000,000.00 USD

    Brief description of the option

    The Duquesa landfill is the central intervention of the Comprehensive Management Program and Sustainable Solid Waste Management in Greater Santo Domingo, financed through the Contract of Loan No. 5678/OC-DR between the Dominican Republic and the Inter-American Development Bank (IDB). In its Component 1, the program contemplates the progressive closure of the open-air site open, accompanied by biogas collection systems, leachate treatment and measures of Technical supervision during the closure and post-closure phases, in order to mitigate emissions of greenhouse gases (estimated at 138,546 tCO₂eq) and prevent pollution of soils and water resources.

    In addition, Component 2 finances the construction of modern infrastructure. disposal and recovery, including landfills, treatment plants and transfer stations. In Duquesa, the installation of an auxiliary cell is planned as transitional measure towards the new final disposal system. In addition, the following will be promoted: recovery of up to 36,000 tons of valuable waste annually and the closure of some 120 hectares of open-air dumps in the intervention area. These actions are integrated climate change adaptation criteria in infrastructure design and contribute directly to the reduction of environmental impacts and the transformation of the model of waste management in Greater Santo Domingo.

    All activities are supported by JICA, IDB, AECID, among others.

    Proposal of methodologies and/or methods for monitoring

    The methodological approach is consistent with the IPCC's 2006 Guidelines for inventories national greenhouse gas emissions.

    Project monitoring will be framed within the guidelines of the IDB, Law 225-20 and the Decree 541-20 on MRV, as well as methodologies from the IPCC 2006 and the Development Mechanism Clean (CDM/UNFCCC). For the closure of the Duquesa landfill and the management of biogas, the following applies: methodology ACM0001 “Consolidated baseline and monitoring methodology for landfill gas “project activities”, used internationally to estimate and verify GHG reductions in projects for the capture, burning and/or energy use of landfill gas.

    Technical-environmental monitoring: monitoring of flow rate and composition of the captured biogas (CH₄, CO₂), verification of avoided emissions with ACM0001, leachate quality control and groundwater, and audit of the auxiliary cell and new infrastructure. This includes the registration of valued waste (36,000 t/year) and closure of areas (120 ha).

    Institutional and social monitoring: periodic progress reports, external audits of environmental and social compliance, and community participation to assess health impacts, employment and quality of life.

    Activities required for the adaptation of the Goal

    ü Implement a biogas collection and combustion/utilization system (ACM0001 methodology - MDL/UNFCCC); ü Build and operate the auxiliary cell in Duquesa as a transition to the new landfill;

    ü Progressively close 120 ha of landfills with cover, drainage and leachate management;

    ü Start up recovery plants with a capacity of 36,000 t/year of waste recovered;

    • Establish an integrated MRV system (reduced tCO2, recovered waste, compliance) environmental); ü Update of Technical Standards (NORDOM);

    ü Strengthen institutional capacities (trust, municipalities, operators);

    ü Formalize inter-institutional agreements for the operation and supervision of infrastructure;

    Develop programs for the inclusion of recyclers and green jobs in the supply chain valorization; ü Promote education campaigns and waste separation at source;

    ü Implement community participation mechanisms for monitoring the closure and post-closure.

    Observations

    Brief description of the option

    The measure consists of the design, construction and operation of comprehensive systems of Collection, treatment and disposal of wastewater in the coastal cities of Boca Chica, La Romana and Higüey, in order to reduce methane (CH₄) and nitrous oxide emissions (N₂O) generated by the improper management of domestic wastewater. The implementation of This infrastructure will improve treatment efficiency and prevent discharges direct to the environment, and optimize sludge management, contributing to the reduction of GHG emissions, while generating co-benefits in public health and environmental quality coastal.

    Proposal of methodologies and/or methods for monitoring

    The DR-L1158 Program and the project identified as a sample are planned as a possible alternative to reduce emissions compared to a business-as-usual scenario from the sector of sanitation, contributing to the reduction target adopted by the country. Within this framework, and with the objective of being able to quantify and compare the possible emissions generated according to different scenarios with and without the project, are developed to The following is the analysis and methodology applied to estimate and quantify the possible emissions avoided and/or generated from the project that makes up the sample. of the Program: the project “Health Barrier for Boca Chica”.

    The methodology and values of [the following] were applied and considered for the calculation of emissions reference suggested by the ECAM tool[1], facilitating calculation and eventual monitoring that could be made from the greenhouse gas emissions generated during the system operation once the works are completed. The tool is compatible with the IPCC-2019 and the most recent methodologies and recommendations suggested for calculating emissions in the urban water and sanitation sector.

    Activities required for the adaptation of the Goal

    Planned activities:

    In this sense, the project analysis foresees the operation of a system made up of approximately:

    • 30 km of master collectors;

    • 250 km of secondary networks and collectors;

    • 8 pumping stations;

    • 7.5 km of pumping lines and;

    • a treatment system to treat the wastewater generated by the design population year 30, anticipating the primary treatment (grill system, sand trap and system of microscreens) with discharge to a submarine outfall 800 meters from the coastline at Punta Caucedo.

    Proposal of methodologies and/or methods for monitoring

    For estimating and monitoring emission reductions associated with water management For residual waste, it is recommended to use methodologies from the Clean Development Mechanism (CDM) approved by the UNFCCC, such as AM0022 – “Grid-connected electricity generation from renewable waste gas” in the case of biogas capture and energy use, or AMS-III.H – “Methane recovery in wastewater treatment”, applicable to collection, treatment and Wastewater disposal with methane capture and flaring. Likewise, the following can be used: simplified methodologies such as AMS-III.I – “Avoidance of methane production in waste- water treatment through controlled anaerobic digestion”, when digesters are established and biogas burning or utilization systems.

    Follow-up should include:

    • Monitoring of treated wastewater flow rates and their BOD (Biochemical Oxygen Demand) load Oxygen).

    • Measurement or estimation of CH₄ captured, burned, or used for energy.

    • Record of electricity consumption in plant operation (to adjust net balance of emissions).

    • Annual report of verifiable GHG reductions under an MRV system consistent with the IPCC Guidelines 2006 (Category 5D – Wastewater Handling).

    Annex 4: Determination of a greenhouse gas baseline (1990–2035)

    Baseline description

    The process of projecting and estimating the baseline of greenhouse gas emissions (GHG) of the Dominican Republic for the period 1990–2035, within the framework of the update of the The Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC 3.0) is based on data analysis historical data from the National GHG Inventory and applies adjusted statistical methods (Growth Rate Compound Annual Growth Rate (CAGR), moving averages, and polynomial equations) to estimate trends future emissions and removals. The projections are developed in a disaggregated manner by the following gases:

    • Carbon Dioxide CO₂,

    • Methane CH₄,

    • Nitrous Oxide N₂O,

    • HFC hydroflurocarbon

    • And CO2 removals

      With the aim of defining trajectories consistent with the economic and technological reality of country, maintaining comparability with the IPCC methods (2006/2019, AR6), which cons- It constitutes a basic tool for the analysis of mitigation scenarios and the evaluation of cohesion. reference of national climate action goals for NDC 3.0, serving as a reference Methodological reference for the national MRV System.

      Methodological Approach

      The process of projecting greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions and removals is It was developed using a statistical-descriptive approach based on the extension of historical series consistent and in the use of trend growth methods, applying formulas derived from IPCC and regional good practices.

      The analysis period comprises 1990–2035 , and is structured in three phases:

    • Phase 1: Cleaning and harmonization of historical data (1990–2022)

      • Official data were used from the National GHG Inventories , carried out by the Ministry of Environment and Natural Resources (MMARN) and the Council National Climate Change and Carbon Market (CNCCMC).

      • The series were expressed in Gg or kt of CO₂ eq to maintain comparability with the reports previous and the basis of the MRV System.

    • Phase 2: Application of projection methods; four models were used to evaluate the future behavior of emissions:

      • Model 1: Polynomial regression equation adjusted to the historical series (least squares).

        or Model 2: Total Growth Rate (total CAGR), considering a single period: 1990–2022.

        or Model 3: Average growth rate (fixed CAGR), considering three periods: 1990–2000, 2000–2010 and 2010–2022.

      • Model 4: Moving growth (5-year moving CAGR) , which reflects recent variability and adjusts trends based on behavior nearest.

    • Phase 3: Validation and selection of the most representative model

      • The quality of fit was assessed using the Coefficient of Determination (R²) indicators, Root Mean Square Error (RMSE) and Mean Absolute Error (MAE) were calculated, and consistency was checked. of the projected trends.

      • The final decision on the preferred model for each gas was based on a combination of greater statistical adjustment and consistency with the country's sectoral evolution.

        Model Selection Criteria

        The process of selecting the projection model was based on a comparative analysis of statistical performance and contextual consistency.

        For each gas, the four defined methods were applied (Equation, Total CAGR, Fixed CAGR and Mobile CAGR) and were evaluated according to their ability to:

    • Adequately represent the observed historical trend.

    • Maintain consistency with the structural behavior of the emitting sources.

    • Provide a robust statistical fit (R² ≥ 0.80).

    • Minimize the mean error (RMSE, MAE) and avoid overfitting.

    • To reasonably reflect the saturation or stagnation effects inherent in the economy national.

    a. Performance indicators

    Table 1 shows the indicators with their description and the corresponding criteria. assessment.

    Table No. 1. Parameters for the evaluation of performance indicators.

    Indicator

    Description

    Evaluation criteria

    R² (Coefficient of determination)

    Evaluates the proportion of variance explained by the model.

    Highest value (R2 > 0.80).

    RMSE (Root Mean Square Error)

    Measures the average magnitude of the absolute error.

    A lower value indicates greater precision.

    MAE (Mean Absolute Error)

    Average of the absolute deviations between observed and estimated values.

    Complementary to RMSE.

    % Average Error

    It allows the deviation to be interpreted in relative terms.

    Values below 5% are acceptable.

    Sectoral coherence

    Visual and contextual adjustment based on the evolution of the economy and source sectors.

    It must align with current growth patterns and policies.

    b. Application of criteria

    During the evaluation, a comparative analysis matrix was constructed for each gas, in which it was The four models were rated based on the previous indicators. The final model was selected. achieved through a combined approach, weighting the results of each method according to its fit and consistency, according to the following general formula:

    Where, Wi represents the weight assigned to each method, determined according to its R² value and consistency. contextual.

    c. Methodological Flow and organization of the analysis in spreadsheets

    This section should be viewed in conjunction with the spreadsheet prepared for the projection process. and baseline assessment which is found as a structured template in the file.

    Each gas (CO₂, CH₄, N₂O, HFCs and Removals) was worked on in three main tabs that reflect the complete flow of the methodological process:

    1. Data: Based on known emissions data.

    2. Data Series: where the data series were completed in annual series (1990-2022)

    3. Polynomial Projections: Where a projection of the series was made according to the equations resulting.

    4. Total Growth Rates: Where the calculation of the Growth Rates for each gas was performed for the period 1990 to 2022.

    5. Total Growth Projections: Where a projection of the series was made taking into account rates resulting totals.

    6. Average growth rate for the periods 1990–2000, 2000–2010 and 2010–2022.

    7. Fixed Growth Projections (10 years): Where a projection of the series was made taking into account rates of the 3 resulting periods, selecting the one from the nearest period.

    8. Growth rate mobile (5-year mobile CAGR) which identifies the growth rate over a 5-year period, sliding year by year to try to absorb the most immediate changes.

    9. Growth Projections 5-year moving CAGR : Where a projection of the series was made taking into account moving rates in 5-year periods resulting from 1990-2022.

    10. D_CO2, D_CH4, D_N2O, D_HCF, D_CO2+ (absorptions): Where the projection of each gas was carried out in Separate sheets for each model considered for their analysis and their evaluation of the R² ≥ 0.80, minimizing the mean error (RMSE, MAE) the best-fitting models were weighted.

    11. Consolidated, which attempts to outline the behavioral possibilities associated with the models.

    12. Combined Projections: The objective of this sheet is to identify the most representative model of the observed trend and validate its predictive capacity.

    This scheme facilitates the traceability of the calculation and allows cross-verification between the results. statistical data and interpretations.

    Based on the methodologies applied (Polynomial, Total CAGR, Fixed CAGR and Moving CAGR), the following was selected the model with the greatest statistical consistency and contextual coherence for each gas.

    i. Carbon Dioxide (CO₂)

    CO₂: It is the main contributor to the total increase in GHGs. Its evolution is due to the increase sustained by electricity demand, the vehicle fleet and thermoelectric generation.

    In graph 1, CO₂ emissions are shown to have been steadily increasing since 1990 (13,842.7 kt CO₂ eq) until 2022 (28,424.58 kt CO₂ eq), with an average annual growth rate (CAGR) of 2.29%. The three methodologies applied, Polynomial Regression (Basic Equation), Total CAGR (1990- 2022) and Mobile CAGR (last 12 years), show high statistical adjustments (R² > 0.90) and Differences less than ±3%. The moving CAGR model (5 years) offers the best level of correlation. with the historical series and adequately captures the long-term structural trend, and reduces year-over-year volatility. The weighted average (ensemble) projects a range of 41,000–47,000. kt CO₂ eq by 2035, doubling the emissions of 1990.

    Best method selected: CAGR mobile (5 years)

    Chart No. 1. Projected behavior of CO2 for the period 1990-2035.

    ii. Methane (CH₄)

    CH₄: A progressive slowdown in growth is observed, explained by improvements in management of waste and reduction in livestock intensity.

    In graph 2, we can see the CH₄ emissions which increased from 149.6 kt CO₂ eq in 1990 to 415.1 kt CO₂ eq in 2022, with phases of rapid growth (1990–2010) and subsequent stabilization (2010–2022, CAGR ≈ −0.7%). The three methods, Polynomial Regression, Total CAGR and Moving CAGR, They maintain R² > 0.90, with intermodel dispersion of less than ±4%. The estimate is based on the CAGR The moving average better represents the non-linearity of the series (growth followed by a plateau), and It softens recent behavior and produces a prudent and realistic projection.

    Best method selected: CAGR Mobile (last 12 years).

    Chart No. 2. Projected behavior of CH4 for the period 1990-2035.

    iii. Nitrous Oxide (N₂O)

    N₂O: It exhibits bimodal behavior; after a drop in the 2000s, it resumes a trend upward trend linked to agricultural fertilization, livestock activity and emissions associated with waste and fuel burning.

    In graph 3, N₂O emissions are observed, which fluctuate significantly due to Variations in agriculture and land use: from 2.74 kt CO₂ eq in 1990 to 7.367 kt CO₂ eq in 2022. The methods applied: Polynomial Regression, Total CAGR and Moving CAGR (last 12 years). They yield low R² values, allowing for better capture of oscillations (peaks and falls) and smoothing the series and resulting in greater long-term stability. The ensemble estimates between 8.0 and 52.0 kt CO₂ eq N₂O in 2035.

    Best method selected: Total CAGR (1990–2022)

    Chart No. 3. Projected behavior of N2O for the period 1990-2035.

    iv. Hydrofluorocarbons (HFC)

    HFC: Exhibits a rapid expansion curve, representative of the technological transition towards alternative refrigerants and the growth of the services sector.

    HFC emissions have been growing rapidly since 2010 (HCFC → HFC transition), reaching 2,360.1 kt CO₂ eq in 2022. An annual growth rate of 1% was applied as it reproduces better the recent dynamics of technological expansion and market behavior.

    v. CO₂ Removals (Absorptions)

    In graph 4, it can be seen that CO₂ removals reflect a sustained decreasing trend, explained by the loss of forest cover and the maturation of natural sinks, which They decreased from 13,600 kt CO₂ eq in 1990 to 10,248 kt CO₂ eq in 2022 (-1.0% annually). The methods The applied methods, Polynomial Regression, Total CAGR and Moving CAGR (last 12 years), yield low R² values. The polynomial model offers the best fit to the sustained downward trend, while the Mobile CAGR mitigates recent declines due to reforestation.
